\subsection{Distance bound for the original Horn problem}
\label{sec:dist_bound_for_orig_horn}

In this section, we prove the following proposition pertaining to the original Horn problem as a warm-up exercise before we prove its generalization for the tetrahedral Horn problem (\cref{thm:approx_tet}) in \cref{sec:dist_bound_for_tet_horn}.
\begin{prop}
    \label{prop:approx_tri}
    Let $(\ea,\eb,\ec)$ be a triple of non-negative eigenvalues satisfying trace condition $\tea + \teb = \tec$.
    If for all pairs of Young diagrams $(\ya,\yb)$ of total size $\sya + \syb = k$, 
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:tri_ab_c_nec_ineq}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb) \leq \sum_{\substack{\ya,\yb\\c^{\yc}_{\ya\yb} > 0}} \vp_{\yc}(\ec),
    \end{equation}
    then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:tri_ab_c_dist_bound}
        \frac{D(\ea,\eb;\ec)}{\tec} \leq O\left(n\sqrt{\frac{\log k}{k}}\right).
    \end{equation}
    where $D(\ea,\eb;\ec)\coloneqq \min_{(A,B) \in \orbit_\ea \times \orbit_\eb} \norm{\eig(A+B)-\ec}_{1}$.
\end{prop}
\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{prop:approx_tri}]
    The proof proceeds is two stages.
    In the first stage we fix an \textit{arbitrary} pair $(\ya,\yb)$ with total size $\sya + \syb = k$ and derive multiple bounds on the product $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)$.
    Then in the second stage, we sum over all such pairs and then obtain the claimed bound on $D(\ea,\eb;\ec)$.

    To begin, we will bound $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)$ in two ways and then apply the eigenvalue separation lemma (specifically \cref{eq:weak_eig_sep}).
    The first bound on $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)$ is obtained from relaxing the assumption in \cref{eq:tri_ab_c_nec_ineq} by considering a Young diagram $\yc^{*}$ of size $k$ which maximizes $\vp_{\yc^{*}}(\ec)$ amoung those $\yc$ such that $c^{\yc}_{\ya\yb }> 0$ for the given $(a,b,c)$.
    As there are at most $\polyk{n-1}$ Young diagrams of size $k$, we conclude
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:first_ab_bound}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb) \leq \polyk{n-1} \vp_{\yc^{*}}(\ec).
    \end{equation}
    The second bound on $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)$ is obtained by considering \cref{cor:random_sum} which states that for any $\yc$ such that $c^{\yc}_{\ya\yb} > 0$,
    \begin{equation}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb) 
        = \frac{\dim V_{\ya}^{n} \ot V_{\yb}^{n}}{c^{\yc}_{\ya\yb} \dim V_{\yc}^{n}}
        \expect_{\substack{(X,Y) \sim \\ \orbit_{\ex} \times \orbit_{\ey}}} 
        \vp^{\ya\yb}_{\yc}(X,Y).
    \end{equation}
    To relax this into a usable bound we (i) bound the dimensions by $\frac{\dim V_{\ya}^{n} \ot V_{\yb}^{n}}{c^{\yc}_{\ya\yb} \dim V_{\yc}^{n}} \leq \dim (V_{\ya}^{n} \ot V_{\yb}^{n}) \leq \polyk{2\binom{n}{2}}$, then (ii) bound the average over $X \in \orbit_{\ex}$ and $Y \in \orbit_{\ey}$ with a maximum, then (iii) apply \cref{cor:row_col_horn_ineqs} to get $\vp^{\ya\yb}_{\yc}(X,Y) \leq \vp_{\yc}(X+Y)$ and then finally (iv) specialize to the optimal $\yc \mapsto \yc^{*}$ to produce
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:second_ab_bound}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb) \leq \polyk{2\binom{n}{2}} \max_{\substack{(X,Y) \in \\ \orbit_\ea \times \orbit_\eb}} \vp_{\yc^{*}}(X+Y).
    \end{equation}
    Then we combine the two bounds on $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)$ from \cref{eq:first_ab_bound} and \cref{eq:second_ab_bound} and apply the eigenvalue separation bound \cref{eq:weak_eig_sep} to obtain
    \begin{align}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb) 
        &\leq \sqrt{\polyk{2\binom{n}{2}} \polyk{n-1} \max_{\substack{(X,Y) \in \\ \orbit_\ea \times \orbit_\eb}} \vp_{\yc^{*}}(X+Y)\vp_{\yc^{*}}(\ec)},\\
        &\leq \sqrt{\polyk{2\binom{n}{2}}\polyk{n-1}}\polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \max_{\substack{(X,Y) \in \\ \orbit_\ea \times \orbit_\eb}}\frac{F(\eig(X+Y), c)^k}{k!}, \\
        \label{eq:ab_intermediate_bound}
        &= \polyk{2\binom{n}{2}+\frac{n-1}{2}}\frac{F(a,b;c)^k}{k!},
    \end{align}
    where $F(a,b;c)$ is defined as the maximum fidelity
    \begin{equation}
        F(a,b;c) \coloneqq \max_{\substack{(X,Y) \in \\ \orbit_\ea \times \orbit_\eb}} F(\eig(X+Y),c).
    \end{equation}

    Now we sum both sides of \cref{eq:ab_intermediate_bound} over \textit{all} pairs $(\ya,\yb)$ with total size $\sya+\syb=k$ to obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{\tec^k}{k!} \leq \polyk{2\binom{n}{2}+\frac{n-1}{2} + 2n} \frac{F(a,b;c)^{k}}{k!},
    \end{equation}
    where the left-hand side follows from the trace condition $\tec=\tea+\teb$ and
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:binomial_trace}
        \sum_{\ell=0}^{k} \sum_{\substack{\ya\vdash \ell\\\yb\vdash k-\ell}} \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb) = \sum_{\ell=0}^{k} \frac{\tea^\ell}{\ell!} \frac{\teb^{k-\ell}}{(k-\ell)!} = \frac{(\tea+\teb)^k}{k!},
    \end{equation}
    whereas the extra factor of $2n$ in the exponent on the right-hand side is obtained because there are at most $\polyk{n}$ Young diagrams with $n$ rows and size at most $k$ and we are summing over a pair $(\ya,\yb)$ with total size $\sya+\syb=k$.
    This establishes a lower bound on $F(a,b;c)$ of the form
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{F(a,b;c)}{\Tr[c]} \geq \left( \frac{1}{\polyk{\frac{2n^2+3n-1}{2}}} \right)^{\frac{1}{k}}
    \end{equation}
    Finally, using \cref{eq:fidelity_vs_distance}, we substitute the maximum normalized fidelity $\frac{F(a,b;c)}{\Tr[c]}$ with a minimum normalized distance to obtain \cref{eq:tri_ab_c_dist_bound} which completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Distance bound for the tetrahedral Horn problem}
\label{sec:dist_bound_for_tet_horn}

The purpose of this section is to prove our main distance bound, \cref{thm:approx_tet}.
Note that \cref{thm:approx_tet} then immediately implies the ``if'' portion of the claim of \cref{thm:iff_tet} by taking $k \to\infty$. 
The ``only if'' portion of \cref{thm:iff_tet} was proven in \cref{cor:tet_eigenvalue_cubic}.
\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:approx_tet}]
    The proof of \cref{thm:approx_tet} mimics the structure of the proof of \cref{prop:approx_tri}.
    In the first stage, we fix an arbitrary triple $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$ with total size $\sya + \syb + \syd = k$ and derive multiple bounds on the product $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$.
    Then in the second stage, we sum over all such triples $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$ and obtain the bound on $D\tightparensixj{\ea & \eb & \ec \\ \ed & \ee & \ef}$.

    We begin by bounding $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$ in two seperate ways and then afterward combine them to apply the eigenvalue separation lemma (specifically \cref{eq:weak_eig_sep}).
    The first bound on $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$ is obtained from relaxing the assumption of the claim that $(\ea,\eb,\ec,\ed,\ee,\ef)$ satisfies \cref{eq:nec_tet} for all triples of Young diagrams $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$.
    By multiplying both sides of \cref{eq:nec_tet} by $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)^{\frac{1}{3}}\vp_{\yd}(\ed)^{\frac{1}{3}}$ we conclude
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:abd_multiplied}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)
        \leq \sum_{\yc,\ye,\yf} \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{\infty}
        \left(
            \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yf}(\ef)
            \vp_{\yc}(\ec)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)
            \vp_{\ye}(\ee)
        \right)^{\frac{1}{3}}
        .
    \end{equation}
    Now let $(\yc^*, \ye^*, \yf^*)$ denote a triple of Young diagrams which maximizes the summand above.
    Note that the summand is only non-zero if the size constraints $\sya+\syb+\syd=\sya + \syf = \syc + \syd = \sye = k$ are satisfied.
    As there are at most $\polyk{3(n-1)}$ such triples satisfying the size constraints (remember $\ya,\yb,\yd$ are fixed), we conclude that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:first_abd_bound}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)
        \leq \polyk{3(n-1)} \norm{\abcdefsymbolcefstarred}_{\infty}
        \left(
            \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yf^*}(\ef)
            \vp_{\yc^*}(\ec)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)
            \vp_{\ye^*}(\ee)
        \right)^{\frac{1}{3}}
        .
    \end{equation}
    The second bound is obtained by considering \cref{cor:random_matrices_tet} which states that for any triple $(\yc,\ye,\yf)$ we have
    \begin{equation}
        \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{2}^{2} \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)
        =
        \frac{\dim (V_\ya^{n} \ot V_{\yb}^{n} \ot V_{\yd}^{n})}{\dim (V_{\ye}^{n})}
        \expect_{\substack{(X,Y,Z) \sim \\ \orbit_{\ex} \times \orbit_{\ey} \times \orbit_{\ez}}}
        \vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z),
    \end{equation}
    To obtain our desired bound on $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$ we make a series of relaxations.
    First we bound the dimensions of the representations by $\frac{\dim (V_\ya^{n} \ot V_{\yb}^{n} \ot V_{\yd}^{n})}{\dim (V_{\ye}^{n})} \leq \dim (V_\ya^{n} \ot V_{\yb}^{n} \ot V_{\yd}^{n}) \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}}$.
    Then we upper bound the average of $\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)$ over $(X,Y,Z) \in \orbit_{\ea} \times \orbit_{\eb} \times \orbit_{\ed}$ with the maximum of the absolute value.
    Together this gives
    \begin{equation} 
        \label{eq:max_abs_value_bound}
        \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{2}^{2} \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed) \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}} \max_{\substack{(X,Y,Z) \in \\ \orbit_{\ea} \times \orbit_{\eb} \times \orbit_{\ed}}}\abs{\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)},
    \end{equation}
    Furthermore, applying \cref{lem:varphi_tet_main_ineq} and \cref{prop:tet_psum_ineq} to the absolute value $\abs{\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)}$ on the right-hand side above produces
    \begin{equation} 
        \label{eq:sup_bound}
        \abs{\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)} \leq \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{\infty}
        \left(\vp_{\ya}(X)\vp_{\yf}(Y+Z)\vp_{\yc}(X+Y)\vp_{\yd}(Z)\vp_{\ye}(X+Y+Z)\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}.
    \end{equation}
    Applying \cref{eq:sup_bound} to \cref{eq:max_abs_value_bound} gives 
    \begin{align} 
        \label{eq:second_abd_bound}
        \begin{split}
            &\norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{2}^{2} \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed) \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}} \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{\infty} 
            \times \cdots \\ &\qquad\cdots \times 
            \max_{\substack{(X,Y,Z) \in\\ \orbit_{\ea} \times \orbit_{\eb} \times \orbit_{\ed}}}
            \left(\vp_{\ya}(X)\vp_{\yf}(Y+Z)\vp_{\yc}(X+Y)\vp_{\yd}(Z)\vp_{\ye}(X+Y+Z)\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    Now we choose $(\yc,\ye,\ef)$ in the above bound to be equal to the optimal choice from $(\yc^*, \ye^*, \yf^*)$ from \cref{eq:first_abd_bound} and multiply the result with \cref{eq:first_abd_bound} and then take a square root to obtain
    \begin{align}
        \label{eq:third_abd_bound}
        \begin{split}
            &\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)
            \leq \polyk{\frac{3}{2}\binom{n}{2} + \frac{3}{2}(n-1)}
            \times \cdots \\ &\qquad\cdots \times 
            \max_{\substack{(X,Y,Z) \in\\ \orbit_{\ea} \times \orbit_{\eb} \times \orbit_{\ed}}}
            \left(
                \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\ya}(X)
                \vp_{\yf^*}(\ef)\vp_{\yf^*}(Y+Z)
                \vp_{\yc^*}(\ec)\vp_{\yc^*}(X+Y)
                \vp_{\yd}(\ed)\vp_{\yd}(Z)
                \vp_{\ye^*}(\ee)\vp_{\ye^*}(X+Y+Z)
            \right)^{\frac{1}{6}}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    Note we have eliminated the $6j$ symbols by using the bound $\norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{\infty}^2 \leq \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{2}^2$.
    We are now in a position to apply the eigenvalue separation lemma (specifically \cref{eq:weak_eig_sep}).
    For each Young diagram $\yx \in \{\ya, \yd, \yc, \ye, \yf\}$ we apply \cref{eq:weak_eig_sep} (noting $\syx \leq k$ for each) to conclude
    \begin{align}
        \sqrt{\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\ya}(X)}
        &\leq \polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \frac{F(\ea, \eig(X))^{\sya}}{\sya!} \\%= \polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \frac{\tea^\sya}{\sya!}, \\
        \sqrt{\vp_{\yd}(\ed)\vp_{\yd}(Z)}
        &\leq \polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \frac{F(\ed, \eig(Z))^{\syd}}{\syd!} \\%= \polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \frac{\ted^\syd}{\syd!}, \\
        \sqrt{\vp_{\yc}(\ec)\vp_{\yc}(X+Y)}
        &\leq \polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \frac{F(\ec, \eig(X+Y))^{\syc}}{\syc!}, \\
        \sqrt{\vp_{\yf}(\ef)\vp_{\yf}(Y+Z)}
        &\leq \polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \frac{F(\ef, \eig(Y+Z))^{\syf}}{\syf!}, \\
        \sqrt{\vp_{\ye}(\ee)\vp_{\ye}(X+Y+Z)}
        &\leq \polyk{\binom{n}{2}} \frac{F(\ee, \eig(X+Y+Z))^{\sye}}{\sye!}.
    \end{align}
    At this stage, we can can combine the bounds for $\ya$ and $\yf$ to obtain an expression involving the same power of $k$:
    \begin{align}
        \sqrt{\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\ya}(X)} \sqrt{\vp_{\yf}(\ef)\vp_{\yf}(Y+Z)} 
        &\leq \polyk{n(n-1)} \frac{F(\ea, \eig(X))^{\sya}}{\sya!}\frac{F(\ef, \eig(Y+Z))^{\syf}}{\syf!}, \\
        &\leq \polyk{n(n-1)} \frac{[F(\ea, \eig(X)) + F(\ef, \eig(Y+Z))]^{k}}{k!}, \\
        &= \polyk{n(n-1)} \frac{F(\ea\oplus \ef, \eig(X\oplus Y+Z))^{k}}{k!},
    \end{align}
    because $\sya + \syf = \sye = k$.
    A similar bound also follows from combining $\yc$ with $\yd$.
    Define the maximum product fidelity as
    \begin{align}
        F\tightparensixj{\ea & \eb & \ec \\ \ed & \ee & \ef} 
        &\coloneqq
        \max_{\substack{(X,Y,Z) \in\\ \orbit_{\ea} \times \orbit_{\eb} \times \orbit_{\ed}}}
        F(\ea\oplus \ef, \eig(X\oplus Y+Z))
        F(\ec\oplus \ed, \eig(X+Y\oplus Z))
        F(\ee, \eig(X+Y+Z)).
    \end{align}
    From \cref{eq:third_abd_bound} and the above fidelity bounds we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)
        \leq \polyk{\frac{3}{2}\binom{n}{2} + \frac{3}{2}(n-1)+ \frac{3}{3}n(n-1)} \frac{1}{k!}F\tightparensixj{\ea & \eb & \ec \\ \ed & \ee & \ef}^{\frac{k}{3}}
    \end{equation}

    Now that we have established an upper bound on $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$ which is uniform in the triple $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$, the next step is to sum this expression over all triples $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$ such that $\sya + \syb + \syd = k$.
    Note that summing $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$ over $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$ with total size $\sya+\syb+\syd=k$ yields the analogue of \cref{eq:binomial_trace}:
    \begin{equation}
        \sum_{\substack{\ya,\yb,\yd \\\sya + \syb + \syd = k}} \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed) = \frac{(\tea + \teb + \ted)^k}{k!} = \frac{\tee^k}{k!}
    \end{equation}
    where we used that the trace conditions hold, i.e., $\tee = \tea + \teb + \ted$.

    As there are at most $\polyk{3n}$ triples Young diagrams $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$ with total size $\sya+\syb+\syd=k$ being summed here, we finally conclude
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{\tee^k}{k!} \leq \polyk{\frac{3}{2}\binom{n}{2} + \frac{3}{2}(n-1)+ \frac{3}{3}n(n-1) + 3n} \frac{1}{k!}F\tightparensixj{\ea & \eb & \ec \\ \ed & \ee & \ef}^{\frac{k}{3}}
    \end{equation}
    or equivalently
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{\tee}F\tightparensixj{\ea & \eb & \ec \\ \ed & \ee & \ef}^{\frac{1}{3}} \geq \left(\frac{1}{\polyk{\frac{7n^2+11n+6}{4}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{k}}.
    \end{equation}
    Finally, using \cref{eq:fidelity_vs_distance}, we can substitute the maximum normalized product of fidelities here with minimum normalized sum of distances (as in \cref{eq:defn_tet_deviation}) to obtain \cref{eq:tri_abd_cef_dist_bound} which completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Asymptotics of the \texorpdfstring{$6j$}{6j} symbols}

In order to prove \cref{cor:asym_6j} is will be useful to have a bound on the quantity $\abs{\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)}$ which directly relates the sextuple of Young diagrams $(\ya,\yb,\yc,\yd,\ye,\yf)$ to the eigenvalues of $(X,Y,X+Y,Z,X+Y+Z,Y+Z)$.
\begin{lem}
    \label{lem:asym_sixj_helper}
    To begin let $X,Y,Z \geq 0$ be positive semidefinite $n\times n$ matrices and let $(\ea,\eb,\ec,\ed,\ee,\ef)$ be the eigenvalues of the matrices $(X, Y, X+Y, Z, X+Y+Z,Y+Z)$.
    Then for all sextuples of Young diagrams, $(\ya,\yb,\yc,\yd,\ye,\yf)$, with sizes satisfying 
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:yd_k_sizes}
        k = \sye = \sya + \syb + \syd = \sya + \syf = \syc + \syd,
    \end{equation}
    we have (assuming $\tee \neq 0$) the following bounds
    \begin{align}
        \label{eq:hex_6j_bound}
        \frac{k!}{\tee^k}\abs{\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)} 
        &\leq \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{\infty}, \\
        \label{eq:hex_rel_ent_bound}
        \frac{k!}{\tee^k}\abs{\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)} 
        &\leq \polyk{n(n-1)} \exp\left(-\frac{k}{4} R_{\ea\eb\ec\ed\ee\ef}^{\ya\yb\yc\yd\ye\yf}\right),
    \end{align}
    where
    \begin{equation}
        R_{\ea\eb\ec\ed\ee\ef}^{\ya\yb\yc\yd\ye\yf} = \kl{\frac{\ya \oplus \yb \oplus \yd}{k}}{\frac{\ea\oplus \eb \oplus \ed}{\tee}} 
        + \kl{\frac{\yc \oplus \yd}{k}}{\frac{\ec \oplus \ed}{\tee}}
        + \kl{\frac{\ya \oplus \yf}{k}}{\frac{\ea\oplus \ef}{\tee}}
        + \kl{\frac{\ye}{k}}{\frac{\ee}{\tee}}.
    \end{equation}
\end{lem}
\begin{proof}
    A combination of \cref{lem:varphi_tet_main_ineq} and \cref{prop:tet_psum_ineq} implies
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:abs_quad_bound}
        \abs{\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(X,Y,Z)} 
        \leq \norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{\infty} \left(\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed) \times \vp_{\yc}(\ec)\vp_{\yd}(\ed) \times \vp_{\ye}(\ee) \times \vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yf}(\ef)\right)^{\frac{1}{4}},
    \end{equation}
    From here we note that each of $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$, $\vp_{\yc}(\ec)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)$, $\vp_{\ye}(\ee)$, and $\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yf}(\ef)$ is upper bounded by $\frac{\tee^k}{k!}$ because they each sum to $\frac{\tee^k}{k!}$ when summing over  the corresponding sets of Young diagrams with sizes given by \cref{eq:yd_k_sizes} (see \cref{eq:binomial_trace}).
    Applying these upper bounds to \cref{eq:abs_quad_bound} (and multiplying by $\frac{k!}{\tee^k}$) gives \cref{eq:hex_6j_bound}.

    For the proof if \cref{eq:hex_rel_ent_bound}, consider the relative entropy bounds arising from eigenvalue estimation \cref{prop:eig_est}:
    \begin{align}
        \frac{k!}{\tee^k}{\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)}
        &\leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}}\exp\left[ - k \kl{\frac{\ya \oplus \yb \oplus \yd}{k}}{\frac{\ea\oplus \eb \oplus \ed}{\tee}}\right], \\
        \frac{k!}{\tee^k}{\vp_{\yc}(\ec)\vp_{\yd}(\ed)}
        &\leq\polyk{2\binom{n}{2}} \exp\left[ - k \kl{\frac{\yc \oplus \yd}{k}}{\frac{\ec \oplus \ed}{\tee}}\right], \\
        \frac{k!}{\tee^k}{\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yf}(\ef)}
        &\leq\polyk{2\binom{n}{2}}\exp\left[ - k \kl{\frac{\ya \oplus \yf}{k}}{\frac{\ea\oplus \ef}{\tee}}\right], \\
        \frac{k!}{\tee^k}{\vp_{\ye}(\ee)}
        &\leq\polyk{\binom{n}{2}}\exp\left[ - k \kl{\frac{\ye}{k}}{\frac{\ee}{\tee}}\right].
    \end{align}
    Applying these bounds to \cref{eq:abs_quad_bound} and using $\norm{\abcdefsymbol}_{\infty} \leq 1$, we obtain \cref{eq:hex_rel_ent_bound}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{cor:asym_6j}]
    First we prove the claimed inverse polynomial lower bound, and then afterwards prove the exponential decay.

    Assume $(\ea,\eb,\ec,\ed,\ee,\ef)$ are the eigenvalues of positive semidefinite matrices $(A,B,C,D,E,F)$ satisfying the tetrahedral constraints.
    Summing $\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(A,B,D)$ over all sextuples of Young diagrams satisfying \cref{eq:yd_k_sizes} produces
    \begin{equation}
        \sum_{\substack{\ya,\yb,\yd \\ \yc,\ye,\yf \\ \sye = k}} \vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(A,B,D) = \frac{\tee^k}{k!}.
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, the sextuple which maximizes the absolute value of $\vp^{\ya\yb\yd}_{\yc\ye\yf}(A,B,D)$ over this $k$-dependent domain of summation, which we denote by $(\ya_k,\yb_k,\yc_k,\yd_k,\ye_k,\yf_k)$, must satisfy
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{k!}{\tee^k}\abs{\vp^{\ya_k\yb_k\yd_k}_{\yc_k\ye_k\yf_k}(A,B,D)} \geq \frac{1}{(k+1)^{6\rank[\ee]}}
    \end{equation}
    where $(k+1)^{6\rank[\ee]}$ is a crude upper bound on the number of terms in the above summation which are non-zero as there are at most $(k+1)^{r}$ Young diagrams with size at most $k$ and depth at most $r$ (note that each of $\vp_{\ya}(\ea), \vp_{\yb}(\eb), \vp_{\yd}(\ed),\vp_{\yf}(\ef), \vp_{\yc}(\ec)$ and $\vp_{\ye}(\ee)$ are respectively only non-zero if the depth of the corresponding Young diagram is at most the rank of the corresponding set of eigenvalues and $\rank[\ee]$ is always the maximum possible rank).
    Therefore, applying \cref{eq:hex_6j_bound} in \cref{lem:asym_sixj_helper} gives
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:n_independent_inverse_poly}
        \norm{\tightcurlysixj{ \ya_k & \yb_k & \yc_k \\ \yd_k &  \ye_k &  \yf_k}}_{\infty} \geq \frac{1}{(k+1)^{6\rank[\ee]}},
    \end{equation}
    while \cref{eq:hex_rel_ent_bound} in \cref{lem:asym_sixj_helper} gives
    \begin{equation}
        \exp\left( -\frac{k}{4} R_{\ea\eb\ec\ed\ee\ef}^{\ya_k\yb_k\yc_k\yd_k\ye_k\yf_k}\right) 
        \geq \frac{1}{(k+1)^{n(n-1)}}\frac{1}{(k+1)^{6\rank[\ee]}}.
    \end{equation}
    Thus we have demonstrated the existence of a sequence of sextuples of Young diagrams for which (i) $\norm{\abcdefsymbolk}_{\infty}$ satisfies the claimed inverse polynomial lower bound, and also (ii) $R_{\ea\eb\ec\ed\ee\ef}^{\ya_k\yb_k\yc_k\yd_k\ye_k\yf_k} \to 0$ which implies the convergence claim:
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{\ya_k}{k} \to \frac{a}{\tee}, \quad
        \frac{\yb_k}{k} \to \frac{b}{\tee}, \quad
        \frac{\yc_k}{k} \to \frac{c}{\tee}, \quad
        \frac{\yd_k}{k} \to \frac{d}{\tee}, \quad
        \frac{\ye_k}{k} \to \frac{e}{\tee}, \quad
        \frac{\yf_k}{k} \to \frac{f}{\tee}.
    \end{equation}
    
    We now prove the exponential decay on $\norm{\abcdefsymbolk}_{\infty}$ when $(\ya_k,\yb_k,\yc_k,\yd_k,\ye_k,\yf_k)$ converges to some non-tetrahedral eigenvalues.
    It will be slightly easier to prove the exponential decay of $\norm{\abcdefsymbolk}_{2}$ which is actually a bit stronger than necessary.
    Without loss of generality, we can assume the Young diagrams $(\ya_k,\yb_k,\yc_k,\yd_k,\ye_k,\yf_k)$ satisfy the size constraints \cref{eq:yd_k_sizes} as before (since otherwise $\abcdefsymbolk = 0$).
    Then from \cref{cor:random_matrices_tet}, we conclude that for \textit{any} eigenvalues $(\ea,\eb,\ed)$,
    \begin{equation}
        \norm{\tightcurlysixj{ \ya_k & \yb_k & \yc_k \\ \yd_k &  \ye_k &  \yf_k}}_{2}^{2} \vp_{\ya_k}(a)\vp_{\yb_k}(b)\vp_{\yd_k}(d)
        = \frac{\dim (V_{\ya_k}^{n} \ot V_{\yb_k}^{n} \ot V_{\yd_k}^{n})}{\dim (V_{\ye_k}^{n})}
        \expect_{\substack{(X,Y,Z) \sim \\ \orbit_{\ea} \times \orbit_{\eb} \times \orbit_{\ed}}}
            \vp^{\ya_k\yb_k\yd_k}_{\yc_k\ye_k\yf_k}(X,Y,Z).
    \end{equation}
    As in the proof of \cref{thm:approx_tet} we first we bound the dimensions of the representations by $\frac{\dim (V_{\ya_k}^{n} \ot V_{\yb_k}^{n} \ot V_{\yd_k}^{n})}{\dim (V_{\ye_k}^{n})} \leq \dim (V_{\ya_k}^{n} \ot V_{\yb_k}^{n} \ot V_{\yd_k}^{n}) \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}}$.
    Then we upper bound the average of $\vp^{\ya_k\yb_k\yd_k}_{\yc_k\ye_k\yf_k}(X,Y,Z)$ with the maximum of the absolute value.
    Together this gives
    \begin{equation} 
        \norm{\abcdefsymbolk}_{2}^{2} \vp_{\ya_k}(\ea)\vp_{\yb_k}(\eb)\vp_{\yd_k}(\ed) \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}} \max_{\substack{(X,Y,Z)\in  \\\orbit_{\ea} \times \orbit_{\eb} \times \orbit_{\ed}}}\abs{\vp^{\ya_k\yb_k\yd_k}_{\yc_k\ye_k\yf_k}(X,Y,Z)}.
    \end{equation}
    As this bound holds for all eigenvalues $(\ea,\eb,\ed)$, we can freely choose, for each $k$, a particular triple of eigenvalues $(\ea^*,\eb^*,\ed^*)$ which ensures
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{k!}{\Tr[\ee^{*}]^k} \vp_{\ya_k}(\ea^*)\vp_{\yb_k}(\eb^*)\vp_{\yd_k}(\ed^*) \geq \frac{1}{\polyk{3n}}.
    \end{equation}
    where we are denoting the sum $\Tr[\ea^{*}]+\Tr[\eb^*]+\Tr[\ed^*]$ with $\Tr[\ee^{*}]$ by a slight abuse of notation as no eigenvalues $\ee^*$ have been choosen here.
    That such a choice can always be made follows from the normalization condition
    \begin{equation}
        \sum_{\substack{\ya,\yb,\yd \\ \sya+\syb+\syd = k}}\vp_{\ya}(\ea)\vp_{\yb}(\eb)\vp_{\yd}(\ed) = \frac{(\tea+\teb+\ted)^k}{k!},
    \end{equation}
    and noting that there are at most $\polyk{3n}$ such triples $(\ya,\yb,\yd)$ of Young diagrams in the sum (indeed it suffices to choose $(\ea^*,\eb^*,\ed^*)$ to maximize $\vp_{\ya_k}(\ea)\vp_{\yb_k}(\eb)\vp_{\yd_k}(\ed)$).
    After this choice is made, we obtain the upper bound
    \begin{equation} 
        \norm{\abcdefsymbolk}_{2}^{2} \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}+3n} \frac{\Tr[\ee^{*}]^k}{k!} \max_{\substack{(X,Y,Z) \in \\\orbit_{\ea^*} \times \orbit_{\eb^*} \times \orbit_{\ed^*}}}\abs{\vp^{\ya_k\yb_k\yd_k}_{\yc_k\ye_k\yf_k}(X,Y,Z)}.
    \end{equation}
    Applying \cref{eq:hex_rel_ent_bound} from \cref{lem:asym_sixj_helper} here produces
    \begin{equation} 
        \norm{\abcdefsymbolk}_{2}^{2} \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}+3n + n(n-1)}  \exp\left( - \frac{k}{4} \min_{\substack{(X,Y,Z)\in \\ \orbit_{\ea^*} \times \orbit_{\eb^*} \times \orbit_{\ed^*}}} R_{\ea^{*}\eb^{*}\ec^{*}\ed^{*}\ee^{*}\ef^{*}}^{\ya_k\yb_k\yc_k\yd_k\ye_k\yf_k}\right).
    \end{equation}
    where $\ec^{*} = \exp(X+Y), \ef^{*} = \eig(Y+Z)$, and $\ee^{*} = \eig(X+Y+Z)$.

    Finally, since it was assumed that $(\ya_k,\yb_k,\yc_k,\yd_k,\ye_k,\yf_k)$ are converging to non-tetrahedral eigenvalues, there must exist a sufficiently large $K$ such that for all $k \geq K$ and all tetrahedral eigenvalues $(\ea',\eb',\ec',\ed',\ee',\ef')$, we have a separation $R_{\ea'\eb'\ec'\ed'\ee'\ef'}^{\ya_k\yb_k\yc_k\yd_k\ye_k\yf_k} \geq r$ for some positive constant $r > 0$. 
    Since $(\ea^{*},\eb^{*},\ec^{*},\ed^{*},\ee^{*},\ef^{*})$ are tetrahedral eigenvalues, we can conclude for such sufficiently large $k \geq K$, $R_{\ea^{*}\eb^{*}\ec^{*}\ed^{*}\ee^{*}\ef^{*}}^{\ya_k\yb_k\yc_k\yd_k\ye_k\yf_k} \geq r$ and therefore
    \begin{equation} 
        \norm{\abcdefsymbolk}_{2}^{2} \leq \polyk{3\binom{n}{2}+3n + n(n-1)}  \exp\left( - \frac{k}{4}r\right),
    \end{equation}
    which proves the desired exponential decay.
\end{proof}
